Use `pi0_pick(\"pick up the bowl on the stove\", max_chunks=16)` and accept the grasp if wrist/agentview show the correct bowl held even when `success:false`. Firm with `set_gripper +1`, preserve yaw with `move_pose`, carry through a mid waypoint, descend low over the plate, make one small left/back correction, and release with a long settle.\n\n## Magic numbers\n`rotate_wrist(delta_yaw=+1.57)` before grasp; after rotating, re-preposition over the stove bowl at z around 1.12 before Pi0.\n`pi0_pick max_chunks=16` (band 14-18) with `lift_thresh=0.05` and `gripper_closed_thresh=0.06`; do not discard the grasp solely because the lift heuristic is false.\nCarry with `move_pose`, `gripper=1`, `target_yaw=+1.57`, `step_clip=0.008` (band 0.006-0.010).\nDescend with `step_clip=0.004` (band 0.003-0.006) to eef z around 1.015 (band 1.012-1.026).\nWinning final correction was one short left/back adjustment before opening; avoid long post-release push chains.\nNEVER omit `gripper=1` on `move_pose` while holding.\nNEVER rotate +1.57 and call Pi0 without re-centering over the stove bowl.\n\n## Failure modes\n| symptom | root cause (A<N>) | fix |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Visibly on/near plate but predicate false | A1 used generic bowl +y compensation and post-release pushes; bowl base stayed outside predicate | Change held geometry and first release pose, not repeated pushes |\n| Wrist-guided centering never reaches overlap | A2/A3 varied x/y after inconsistent rim hooks | Change grasp/orientation or measure the current held offset |\n| Yaw change still releases beside plate | A4 used +yaw but release xy was not compensated enough | Preserve +yaw and use a lower, plate-biased release |\n| Adjacent setdown plus lateral push misses | A5 low side pushes moved the bowl but did not climb/seat over plate rim | Prefer direct low release from held state |\n| Measured offset under-aims front/right | A6/A7 offsets varied by grasp and top silhouette misled placement | Bias toward bowl base support point and verify low overlap before release |\n| Extreme xyz with vertical hook still misses | A8 held offset varied larger than prior estimate | Change orientation, not only xyz |\n| -1.57 yaw worsens alignment | A9 side-on carry put plate left/back of bowl in wrist view | Use opposite yaw sign or return to vertical |\n| +1.57 yaw drifts off target before grasp | A10 rotation moved wrist over ramekin/plate | After rotation, explicitly move_to the stove-bowl anchor before Pi0 |\n\n## Re-localization per scene\nTarget bowl: find the patterned black/white/yellow-rim bowl sitting on the gray stove cook region in agentview_high. Reject the identical bowl on the cabinet by the task relation. Text segmentation can choose the wrong duplicate; manual pixels or point prompts on the stove bowl are safer.\nPlate: find the white ceramic disc with red concentric rim rings, not the gray stove burner or silver ramekin. Sample interior/ring pixels and use the midpoint/median as the placement anchor.\nRelation landmarks: stove is the gray metal square/circular cook region under the target bowl; cabinet bowl is elevated on the dark cabinet and is a distractor; ramekin is a small silver cup near the plate and can enter wrist view after +yaw rotation.\nThis run's absolute coordinates are counter-examples only: stove-bowl agentview median around [-0.178,-0.107], plate around [0.093,0.176]. Do not cache them across seeds.\n\n## Fragility flags\nMost fragile step: +yaw pre-grasp rotation changes EEF xy, so Pi0 will ground on the wrong neighborhood unless the EEF is moved back over the stove bowl before the pick. Fallback: if +yaw grasp is not visually held, reset and try the same yaw with a slightly lower/closer re-preposition rather than changing the plate placement first.\n\n## Difficulty and reliability\nSolved on attempt 11 after ten failed attempts. Expected single-shot reliability is moderate only if the +yaw re-center and low compensated release are reproduced; direct vertical-hook placement and post-release pushes were low reliability in this scene.\n\n## Cross-refs\n[[pregrasp-yaw-recenter]]\n[[visual-over-pick-heuristic]]\n[[post-grasp-yaw-offset]]\n"

My task language: "pick up the black bowl on the wooden cabinet and place it on the plate". The seed-0 memory is a different language ("on the stove"). So target = the bowl on the dark cabinet at image left.

Hmm, but careful: this could be the "swap" variant where the object positions are swapped. The cell is suite libero_spatial, task 9, seed 0. The output dir path is libero_spatial_t9_s0. The memory says spatial_task_t9_s0 language was "on the stove". My language differs → maybe this instance is the "swap" version? Or the environment randomizes language? Actually in LIBERO spatial, each task_id has a fixed language. Hmm, but the swap perturbation changes language to swap objects. Under swap, the language would be like "pick up the black bowl on the wooden cabinet and place it on the plate" if stove bowl ↔ cabinet bowl swapped... Actually the swap perturbation swaps object initial positions between... hmm.

Anyway: my language is authoritative. The target is the bowl on the wooden cabinet.

Important nuance: since language refers to the cabinet, maybe the scene was configured so that the "stove" bowl got swapped to appear near the cabinet. But looking at the image: one bowl clearly on top of the dark cabinet (elevated), another bowl on the metallic square. The metallic square — what is it? In libero_spatial scenes, there's a "flat stove" fixture (the metal square with circular cook region). Yes: in the high-res image, the metal square under the middle bowl has a gray circular region = stove cook region. So mid bowl = "on the stove". Left bowl = "on the wooden cabinet".

The plate: center approx (735, 800)? In high-res: the plate spans rows ~660-800, cols ~715-895. Center ~ (735, 805).

Let me back-project. I'll do several calls in parallel.

Also need table height. Let me sample table pixels.

Note: back_project returns surface point; for the bowl top surface z would be ~table+bump. For the bowl on cabinet, z would be cabinet height + bowl height (elevated) — which confirms the relation.

Let's go.

Now the wrist geometry is clear. Mapping: wrist image cols ↔ world y (col 300 → y=-0.2115, col 715 → y=-0.3396). So col increases → y decreases. Wrist image rows ↔ world x (row 500 → x=0.0944, row 845 → x=-0.0044). So row increases → x decreases.

Bowl rim extremes:
- col 300 (y=-0.2115) — bowl near rim (in y+ direction)
- col 715 (y=-0.3396) — far rim in y-
- row 500 (x=0.0944) — far rim in x+
- row 845 (x=-0.0044) — near rim in x-

Center: y = (-0.2115 + -0.3396)/2 = -0.2756. x = (0.0944 + -0.0044)/2 = 0.045.
Diameter: y: 0.128, x: 0.0988. Hmm y-span 0.128 vs x-span 0.099 — asymmetric. That's because the col-300 measurement may have hit the rim edge incorrectly (maybe I hit the yellow rim on the near side which is upslope). Or the row-500 point was slightly inside the rim.

The bowl in the wrist image: it looks like an ellipse spanning cols 295-720 (width 425) and rows 490-860 (height 370). If the aspect should be circular in world, and from mapping 1 col ≈ 0.00031 m and 1 row ≈ 0.000286 m: width 425*0.00031=0.132, height 370*0.000286=0.106. Hmm.

The apparent ellipse is elongated along cols → the projection. Well, whatever. The rim in wrist image: the yellow rim edge. Looking at the image, the visible bowl is a circle-ish shape with the yellow rim, spanning roughly cols 290-720 and rows 490-865. The center of that ≈ (row 678, col 505) → which we back-projected: (0.039, -0.2664, 1.1338). But hold on — from the extremes-derived center we got (0.045, -0.276). And the direct center pixel gave (0.039,-0.266).

Discrepancy in y ~1cm. Let's include the rim bias: the center pixel is the interior bottom (z=1.134), which is displaced from true center due to perspective. The extents give (0.045,-0.276).

Combined estimate: bowl center ≈ (0.043, -0.272). Bowl outer diameter ~0.115 (that's a big bowl... akita_black_bowl is ~0.10-0.11 diameter). Rim top z: at the rim points we got z 1.13-1.18. The rim highest points ~1.178. Interior bottom 1.134. So the bowl height ≈ rim 1.178, base 1.09? No — cabinet top was at 1.09. Hmm, rim - base = 0.088.

Hmm wait, cabinet top z: I sampled (0.0035,-0.177,1.0918) at agentview row 500 col 250. That was in the dark region — but is the cabinet top at z=1.09? Looking at the wrist image, the cabinet top (dark) extends beyond the bowl. The bowl sits on it. Bowl rim at 1.178, and if bowl height 0.088 then base at 1.09. Consistent: cabinet top = 1.09. 

Now important: the bowl base z=1.09, rim z=1.178. So the eef should descend and grasp the rim from above. Eef yaw currently ~0 (quat 0.9995, -0.011, 0.021, 0.021 → nearly identity, tiny yaw). With identity quat, the gripper fingers point along ±y (robot y) — i.e., the finger opening axis is along y. Bowl at y=-0.272, x=0.043.

For a rim grasp with fingers opening along y: the fingers would close onto the rim points at (x=0.043, y=-0.272±0.055). That's actually a reasonable grasp — the two fingers straddle the bowl along the y axis and pinch the far and near rim. Wait no: fingers close toward each other along the y axis; each finger contacts the rim on opposite sides of the bowl along y. Fingers would need to descend to below rim height. Rim z=1.178 and the fingers would pinch the rim's outer walls at ±0.055 y.

If instead yaw=+1.57, fingers point along x, straddling the bowl along x: contacts at x=0.043±0.055.

Given the memory recipe used +1.57 yaw for the bowl grasp, and rotating caused drift. Hmm. Since current yaw is ~0 and the eef is already centered over the bowl, let me just attempt pi0_pick from here without rotating. Pi0 was trained on this task; "pick up the black bowl on the wooden cabinet" should be a well-covered phrase.

Actually, one concern: the open gripper span is 7.8cm (qpos ±0.039). Bowl diameter ~0.11 → fingers can't straddle outside the bowl; they must descend INTO the bowl interior and pinch the inside walls, or grip the rim from behind. Pi0 handles this typically by descending into the bowl and grasping the rim between fingers... Actually LIBERO bowl grasps typically work by the gripper closing on the rim: one finger inside, one outside. This requires the eef to be offset from the bowl center by ~half the rim radius plus a bit, i.e., eef at bowl center + ~0.05 in one direction so that one finger lands inside and the other outside.

Given the ambient success: memory says "BOWL: eef_y = plate_y + 0.045" for placement compensation, not grasp.

Practical approach: use pi0_pick from the current centered position, and if it fails, offset.

The bowl is tilted though (it looks tipped, hanging by a hooked rim). Tipped bowl → the placement will be uncertain: releasing a tilted bowl onto a plate may cause it to land upright if it's resting on the plate... a bowl released from a small height will settle, likely right itself-ish or land on its base. If it's tipped significantly, it may land on its side (bad).

Let me look at the agentview image again more closely: the held bowl at (row~510, col~800) appears as an ellipse tilted; we see the bowl interior partially with the outer wall on the right side. It's like the bowl hangs at an angle of maybe 30-40°. Hmm.

Actually, better plan: The goal predicate for libero_spatial "bowl on plate" is: bowl's xy within plate region and bowl bottom above plate... Let's keep it simple: carry the bowl above the plate, lower it nearly to the plate (z ~0.95-0.98), and release with a long settle. Now clearer.

Wrist at step 4: plate center pixel (455,415) → (0.0543, 0.2189, 0.9067). Bowl pixels (700,300) → (0.0342, 0.2063, 1.1562) and (600,150) → (0.0556, 0.2316, 1.1592).

Interesting: the wrist bowl points are nearly at the plate center xy! (0.034,0.206) and (0.056,0.232) — those are bowl surface points at z≈1.156. Hmm. But those might be the far/top part of the bowl, not the center.

Agentview bowl pixels: (0.0812,0.2056,1.2109), (0.0715,0.2438,1.1904), (0.0638,0.1775,1.2129). These are bowl surface points at z≈1.19-1.21.

Eef is at (0.0275, 0.1651, 1.191).

The bowl is big and tilted. Its apparent footprint in agentview: cols 790-965, rows 370-475 → x from ~0.06 to ~0.09, y from ~0.17 to ~0.25. Center ≈ (0.075, 0.21).

Hmm, so the bowl is roughly at x=0.075, y=0.21; the plate center (0.041, 0.219). So the bowl is ~3.4cm in +x from the plate center. Meaning it's close! But the bowl z is high (1.19-1.21 = raised) — need to descend ~0.25.

Wait, the bowl center (0.075,0.21) — how does that sit relative to eef (0.0275,0.165)? Offset = (+0.048, +0.045). Hmm that's a big offset, ~6.6cm diagonal. The bowl hangs off to the +x,+y side of the gripper. That matches this grasp: the gripper pinched a rim, so the bowl hangs off-axis.

Since the plate is at (0.041,0.219) and bowl center at (0.075,0.21) the bowl is basically over the plate already (dx=0.034 in +x, dy=-0.009). Actually the plate radius 0.06. If the bowl center is 3.4cm from the plate center, part of the bowl is beyond the plate edge. Need correction of ~-0.034 in x.

But also the bowl is tilted. Descending and dropping from a moderate height gives it a chance to settle.

However the bowl's z: at 1.19-1.21, its lowest point? The bowl is tilted, its bottom edge maybe at 1.14. The plate is at 0.907. So we need to descend ~0.23.

Let me be careful about how the bowl is oriented: In the agentview the bowl looks tilted maybe 20-30° counterclockwise? From the wrist image the bowl interior faces up-left. Honestly, dropping onto the plate from ~4-6cm height would likely land it right side up since the bowl's base is flat and heavy.

Plan: shift eef by (-0.034, +0.009) → target (0.0275-0.034, 0.1651+0.009) = (-0.0065, 0.174). Then descend to z such that the bowl bottom touches the plate: bowl bottom currently ≈1.14? Let's estimate: bowl center z ~1.19 (rim top ~1.21, interior 1.156). The bowl's base is probably ~1.16 (since the bowl is ~6-8cm tall and rim at 1.21 → base at 1.13-1.15). To put the base at plate top 0.91, need to descend by 1.14-0.91 = 0.23 → eef z 1.191-0.23 = 0.96.

Hmm but if the bowl hangs ~0.03-0.05 below the eef... Let me measure the bowl's lowest point in the agentview: The bowl bottom in image at row ~475? Actually the bowl's silhouette bottom near row 475-490 → z ~1.16?

To be safe: descend in steps, verifying the bowl's lowest z via wrist/agentview until the base is at ~0.94-0.96, then release and let it settle 1-2cm. Better: descend to eef z=0.98 first, measure, then adjust.

Also risk of the bowl colliding with the ramekin! The ramekin is at (row 490, col 730) in agentview = ? It's near x~0.12,y~0.10 maybe. Not in the way (we're at y 0.17-0.22).

Let me first do the xy correction at current height: move_pose to (-0.006, 0.174, 1.19), then verify.

Actually, careful about being too aggressive: the offset may shift when moving. I'll move, measure, then descend.

Actually, maybe simpler: since bowl center is only 3.4cm off the plate center in +x and the plate radius is 0.06 while the bowl radius is 0.055... An "On" predicate in LIBERO typically compares the bowl's xy to the plate's xy region: I believe it's implemented as: the object's position (its center) must be within the plate's radius (or the bounding box). Actually LIBERO's On(top_obj, bottom_obj) checks that the top object's bottom is above the bottom object's top and their xy overlap sufficiently in the horizontal projection... In the LIBERO code, `On` is evaluated by checking if the object's xy is contained in the bottom object's xy rectangle (bbox) using the visual bbox? For libero, `_get_object_positions`/predicate "on" checks contact and that the object's projection lies within the plate's horizontal extent (using the object's bbox corners? It uses the position of the object and checks whether it's inside the "region" of the other object defined by xy bbox of the mesh).

Better to center the bowl on the plate as well as possible. So do the correction.

Let me do xy correction: move_pose to (-0.006, 0.174, 1.19). Δ = (-0.034, +0.009). Small.

外部DeepSeek原记录 thinking
Region mode gives center (0.0117, 0.2239) and median (0.03, 0.225). Which is the bowl's silhouette center? The region I chose may be slightly off (the bowl may extend outside that window). The "center_xyz" is the midpoint of the pixel window's extent in world coords, the median is the median of all valid points.

Center of the window: (0.0117, 0.2239); median (0.03, 0.225). Difference in x means the window isn't covering the bowl symmetrically — the bowl's high-z interior region is at higher x.

Plate center: (0.041, 0.219).

So bowl needs to shift ~+0.015..+0.03 in x. Small. Given a plate radius of 0.06 and the bowl radius ~0.055, ~2cm error leaves the bowl mostly over the plate. That's likely acceptable for the On predicate.

Rather than iterate more on xy (each iteration adds risk of the bowl slipping), let's continue descending. But I should double check the descent so far: I commanded z 1.05 and it reached 1.0593 (final_dist 0.0093 — within tol). Good.

Current bowl surface z ~1.03-1.10. The bowl's lowest point? The bowl hangs with rim... Let's estimate bowl base: if the bowl interior bottom is at ~1.05 and bowl wall/base thickness small, the bowl's outside bottom ≈1.03. To rest on the plate (0.907), descend 0.12 more → eef z ≈ 0.94. Hmm, but that assumes the bowl bottom is 0.02 below the interior bottom.

Actually the topmost bowl point (rim) is at ~1.10 and the lowest ~1.03 → bowl is fairly level now with height ~0.07 (rim 1.10, base 1.03). So base - plate top = 1.03-0.907 = 0.12. Descend eef by 0.12 → z = 0.94. But careful: would the gripper fingers collide with the plate? The plate is thin; the fingers are closed around the bowl rim. At eef z 0.94, the fingers' bottom would be around... the fingers extend ~0.03 below eef? Hmm, eef_pos is between the fingers roughly at the palm. If the bowl base is at 1.03 when the eef is at 1.059, then the bowl bottom is 0.03 below the eef. At eef z 0.94, the bowl bottom would be 0.91 = plate top. 

So target eef z ≈ 0.945 for the bowl to rest on the plate — but that's tight. Better to descend to z=0.97 (bowl bottom ~0.94, i.e., 3cm above the plate) and release; the bowl drops 3cm onto the plate. It's a flat-bottomed bowl; a 3cm drop should land it flat. Also add slight +x correction.

Also to help align, I'll shift +x by 0.015 and y by 0.

Let's descend to (0.018, 0.222, 0.97). Δ from (0.0032,0.1742,1.0593): Δx 0.015, Δy 0.048, Δz -0.089. Hmm, dy=0.048: the plate is at y=0.219 and the eef is at 0.174, so the bowl hangs offset in +y by ~0.045. Consistent.

Careful about a large simultaneous move; step_clip 0.005 with max_steps 160 → 0.8m of travel budget, fine.

Then measure again, then release.

Actually — important: does the "On" predicate in LIBERO require the bowl to be released/touching? Typically `On` = object B on object A: checks that the projection of B onto A's surface is within A's bounds and the vertical positions are close (contact). Releasing then settling should satisfy it.

Let's do the descent.

Hmm but earlier at step 6 the offset measurement said bowl center (0.012,0.224) with eef (0.003,0.174) → offset (+0.009,+0.05). At step 7 eef (0.0178,0.2221), bowl center (0.028,0.262) → offset (+0.010,+0.040). Consistent offset ≈ (+0.01,+0.045).

So for bowl center = plate center (0.045,0.216): eef = (0.035, 0.171).

Also the bowl z: at step 7 the bowl surfaces are at z≈0.985 while the eef is at 0.979. Hmm — the bowl surface top at 0.985 which is above the eef? That's the far rim, higher than the eef. So the bowl's base: bowl height 0.07 → base 0.915. That's only 8mm above the plate top (0.907)! So the bowl is nearly resting on the plate already (or nearly touching). Actually maybe the bowl hangs at an angle and its bottom is lower than that.

Hmm, but moving laterally while the bowl nearly touches the plate edge risks dragging/catching the plate. The bowl base at 0.915 and plate rim at ~0.92? Plate thickness: plate top 0.907 is measured on the plate's surface (which for a plate is the rim top). If the bowl's base is at 0.915, it's 8mm above the plate rim. Moving laterally 5cm at that height could scrape the plate rim.

Safer sequence: lift up 4cm, translate laterally to align, then descend, then release.

When the bowl center is 4cm off the plate center in y, the bowl's base (radius ~0.035 for the base ring) would be at y 0.22-0.29 — the plate spans y 0.156-0.276. So the bowl's base partially overhangs by ~1.5cm. Might still satisfy the On predicate but risky.

Better fix it. Do the lift-translate-descend.

Release at eef (0.036,0.168,0.998) with bowl center (0.044,0.226) \\u2248 plate center fired the On(plate) predicate after 3 steps \\u2192 terminated:true. Audit and recipe written to the episode directory.\"\n}"
}
